the NLO KK longitudinal and Goldstone amplitudes

(\delta\mathcal{T}_{L}^{\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{5}^{\prime})

are both modified as in Eq.(
5.30
),
the residual term is also modified as
\,\mathcal{T}_{v}^{\prime}\,
accordingly.
Hence, the NLO KK GAET identity (
5.31
) is equivalent to
its original form (
5.29a
), which means that the
gauge symmetry of the KK YM theory is still retained
by the identity (
5.31
).

With the double-copy construction, we can justify the size
of the GRET residual term

\,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}\!=\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2}E^{0})\,

from the KK GAET residual term

\,\mathcal{T}_{v}\!\!=\!\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2}/E^{2})\,
,
where
\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{v}\hskip-0.85358pt
is well understood.
We will demonstrate that
the connection between
sizes
of the two residual terms

\,\mathcal{T}_{v}\!=\!\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2}/E^{2})\,
and

\,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}\!\!=\!\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2}E^{0})\,
is a general prediction
of the double-copy construction and does not depend on details
of the construction.

Constructing KK Scattering Amplitudes
and GRET by Double-Copy

For the compactified 5d YM gauge theory and compactified 5d GR theory,
we expect the double-copy correspondence:

\displaystyle A_{n}^{a\mu}\otimes\!A_{n}^{a\nu}\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\,h^{\mu\nu}_{n}\,,

(5.32a)

\displaystyle A_{n}^{a5}\otimes\!A_{n}^{a5}\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\,h^{55}_{n}\,,

(5.32b)

\displaystyle A_{n}^{a\mu}\otimes\!A_{n}^{a5}\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\,h^{\mu 5}_{n}\,.

(5.32c)

It is instructive to note that the physical spin-2 KK graviton field

h^{\mu\nu}_{n}
arises from the double-copy of spin-1 KK gauge fields

A_{n}^{a\mu}\otimes\!A_{n}^{a\nu}
.
On the other hand,
the
A_{n}^{a5}
is the would-be KK Goldstone boson
in the compactified 5d YM gauge theory,
and the double-copy counterparts
h^{55}_{n}(=\phi_{n})

and
h^{\mu 5}_{n}
just correspond to the scalar KK Goldstone boson
and vector KK Goldstone boson in the compactified 5d GR.
From Eq.(
5.32a
) , we further expect
the double-copy correspondence between the (helicity-zero)
longitudinal KK graviton and KK gauge boson:

\,A_{L}^{an}\otimes\!A_{L}^{an}\!\longrightarrow h_{L}^{n}\,
.
We observe that in the high energy limit the longitudinal KK gauge
boson
A_{L}^{an}\!=\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}A^{an}_{\mu}\,
has its
polarization vector
\,\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\!\!\sim\!k^{\mu}/M_{n}\,
,
and the longitudinal KK graviton

\,h_{L}^{n}\!=\!\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}h^{n}_{\mu\nu}\,
has its polarization tensor

\,\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}\!\!\sim\!k^{\mu}k^{\nu}/M_{n}^{2}\,
.
Thus, we have

\,\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}\!\!\sim\!\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\epsilon_{L}^{\nu}\,

in the high energy limit,
which also makes the longitudinal correspondence
(
\,A_{L}^{an}\otimes\!A_{L}^{an}\!\longrightarrow h_{L}^{n}\,
)
well expected. The demonstration of the double-copy
correspondence between the longitudinal KK gauge boson
amplitudes and the longitudinal KK graviton amplitudes
is much more nontrivial than the above relation between
the on-shell longitudinal polarization vector/tensor,
as we will analyze further in this subsection.

In this subsection, we will first demonstrate that
a double-copy construction from the KK gauge theory amplitudes
to the KK graviton amplitudes at the leading order (LO) of the
high energy expansion, which corresponds to the limit

\,M_{n}/E\!\rightarrow\!0\,
. We find that such leading order amplitudes
are
mass-independent
and their kinematic Jacobi identities
(
5.15
) hold, in addition to the massless Mandelstam
relation
\,s_{0}\!+t_{0}\!+u_{0}\!=0\,
.
Thus, we will first extend the conventional double-copy
method
[
24
]
[
25
]
to the LO amplitudes
in our 5d KK theory and demonstrate how it works quantitatively.

We note that
the (helicity-zero) longitudinal KK gauge bosons
A_{L}^{an}

and longitudinal KK gravitons
h_{L}^{n}
are truly distinctive
in the KK theory because they do not exist in the commonly studied
massless YM gauge theory or massless GR.
Also, in the limit
M_{n}\!\rightarrow\!0
,
the KK Goldstone bosons
A_{5}^{an}

and
\phi^{n}(=\!h^{n}_{55})
both become massless and correspond to
the physical degrees of freedom.
But, it is important to observe that according to the KK GAET
(cf. section
5.1
)
[
8
]
[
9
]

and GRET (sections
3
-
4
),
the leading scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gauge
bosons (KK gravitons) equal the corresponding amplitudes
of the KK Goldstone bosons and are mass-independent
(which correspond to the limit
M_{n}^{2}/E^{2}\!\rightarrow\!0

under high energy expansion).
Hence, we can construct a double-copy from the
leading longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitudes of
\mathcal{O}(E^{0})

to the corresponding longitudinal KK graviton amplitudes
of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
, in parallel to the double-copy construction
between the KK Goldstone amplitudes in the KK YM theory and KK GR.
The KK Goldstone amplitudes are much simpler
due to the absence of any nontrivial
energy-cancellations in the KK Goldstone amplitudes.
Furthermore, since the compactified KK theories
have very different Feynman rules from
the 4d massless gauge theory or massless GR as commonly studied,
the double-copy realization in the KK theory is far from
obvious even for the leading order amplitudes before explicit
demonstration. For instance, there are highly nontrivial and
intricate energy-cancellations in the longitudinal KK gauge boson
scattering amplitudes
[from
\mathcal{O}(E^{4})
down to
\mathcal{O}(E^{0}
)]
[
8
]
and
in the (helicity-zero) longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitudes
[from
\mathcal{O}(E^{10})
down to
\mathcal{O}(E^{2}
)]
[
15
]
,
all these do not exist in the 4d massless gauge theory and massless GR.

We inspect the structures of the KK longitudinal gauge boson
scattering amplitude (
5.14a
)
and the KK corresponding Goldstone boson scattering amplitude
(
5.14b
) in the compactified 5d YM gauge theory
under the high energy expansion.
We see from Eqs.(
5.7
) and
(
5.8
)-(
5.9
)
that under high energy expansions, the leading amplitudes

(\mathcal{T}_{0L}\,,\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{05})

are of
\,\mathcal{O}(E^{0})\,
and
mass-independent,

while the subleading amplitudes

(\delta\mathcal{T}_{L}\,,\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{5})
are of

\,\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2}/E^{2})\,
and vanish in the massless limit
M_{n}\!\rightarrow\!0
.
We have formally expressed these leading amplitudes in the form
the massless gauge theories with pole factors
(s_{0},\,t_{0},\,u_{0})

in the denominator of each channel, even though these poles
are no longer real poles under the current high energy expansion.
For the current study of the 5d KK YM gauge theories and 5d KK GR,
we present an extended formulation
of the conventional BCJ double-copy method
of the massless gauge theories
[
24
]
[
25
]
,

by making the high energy expansion with

\,M_{n}^{2}/E^{2}\!\ll\!1

under which all the nonzero KK mass-poles are removed,
and the mass-dependent contributions can be treated
order by order.

From the numerators of the amplitudes
(
5.14a
)-(
5.14b
),
we see that the kinematic factors
(\mathcal{N}_{s},\,\mathcal{N}_{t},\,\mathcal{N}_{u})

and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u})
may be viewed as

dual to
the color factors
(\mathcal{C}_{s},\,\mathcal{C}_{t},\,\mathcal{C}_{u})

according to the conventional double-copy method in the massless
gauge theories
[
24
]
[
25
]
.
Thus, we attempt to construct the elastic scattering amplitude

\,\mathcal{M}[h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}]\,

of the longitudinal KK gravitons
and the gravitational KK Goldstone boson amplitude

\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]\,

from the corresponding longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude

\,\mathcal{T}[A^{an}_{L}A^{bn}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cn}_{L}A^{dn}_{L}]

and the KK Goldstone boson amplitude

\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A^{an}_{5}A^{bn}_{5}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cn}_{5}A^{dn}_{5}]
,
respectively.
We realize an extended double-copy construction
for the 5d KK YM gauge theory
and 5d KK GR by the following replacement:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{s},\,\mathcal{C}_{t},\,\mathcal{C}_{u})\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ (\mathcal{N}_{s},\,\mathcal{N}_{t},\,\mathcal{N}_{u})\,,

(5.33a)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{s},\,\mathcal{C}_{t},\,\mathcal{C}_{u})\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ (\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u})\,.

(5.33b)

Applying this duality replacement to the scattering amplitudes
of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and KK Goldstone bosons
in Eqs.(
5.14a
)-(
5.14b
) and
Eqs.(
5.8
)(
5.13c
)-(
5.13d
),
we first construct the corresponding scattering amplitudes of
the longitudinal KK gravitons and gravitational
KK Goldstone bosons, to the nonzero leading contributions
of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
in the high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}]\,

\displaystyle=c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\!\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0})^{2}}{s_{0}}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0})^{2}}{t_{0}}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0})^{2}}{u_{0}}\!\,\right]\!,

(5.34a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]\,

\displaystyle=c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\!\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0})^{2}}{s_{0}}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0})^{2}}{t_{0}}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0})^{2}}{u_{0}}\!\,\right]\!,

(5.34b)

where the overall coefficient
c_{0}
is a conversion constant
due to replacing the gauge coupling
\hskip 0.85358ptg\hskip 0.85358pt

by gravitational coupling

\kappa
. The constant
c_{0}
is not known a priority
before a unified UV theory of gauge and gravitational forces
becomes available.

Then, substituting Eqs.(
5.8a
)-(
5.8c
)
into Eqs.(
5.34a
)-(
5.34b
),
we explicitly reconstruct the longitudinal KK graviton
scattering amplitude and the gravitational KK Goldstone
scattering amplitude as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle=\left(\!-\frac{\,9c_{0}g^{2}\,}{\,4\,}\!\right)\!\left[\,\!\frac{\,(3+\cos^{2}\!\theta)^{2}\,}{\sin^{2}\!\theta}\!\,\right]\!s_{0}

(5.35a)

\displaystyle=\left(\!-\frac{\,9c_{0}g^{2}\,}{\,16\,}\!\right)\!\left[\left(7\!+\cos 2\theta\right)^{2}\!\csc^{2}\!\theta\,\right]\!s_{0}

(5.35b)

\displaystyle=\left(\!-\frac{\,9c_{0}g^{2}\,}{\,4\,}\!\right)\!\!\left[\frac{\,(s_{0}^{2}+t_{0}^{2}+u_{0}^{2})^{2}\,}{s_{0}\hskip 1.5ptt_{0}\hskip 1.5ptu_{0}}\right]\!,

(5.35c)

where we have dropped the mass-dependent subleading term
of
\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2})
which is much smaller than the above
leading
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
amplitude in the high energy scattering.

Strikingly, we find that our above
leading amplitudes of the longitudinal KK graviton
and the gravitational KK Goldstone boson
in Eq.(
5.35
),
as constructed by the double-copy method, perfectly agree to
the gravitational KK Goldstone amplitude (
4.22
)
at
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})

which we computed directly
from the KK theory of compactified 5d GR.

Eq.(
5.35
) also explicitly
establishes the
equivalence
between the longitudinal
KK graviton amplitude and the corresponding gravitational KK
Goldstone boson amplitude.
In fact, we can demonstrate this equivalence in
a more elegant and transparent way,
by making use of the relation (
5.17
).
With this, we can express the KK graviton amplitude (
5.34a
)
in terms of the gravitational KK Goldstone boson amplitude:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle=c_{0}g^{2}\!\left\{\!\left[\,\!\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0})^{2}}{s_{0}}\!+\!\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0})^{2}}{t_{0}}\!+\!\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0})^{2}}{u_{0}}\!\,\right]\!\!-8c_{\theta}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0}\!+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0}\!+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0})\!+16c_{\theta}^{2}(s_{0}\!+t_{0}\!+u_{0})\right\}\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}],

(5.36)

where in the last step we have made use of the
kinematic Jacobi identity (
5.15b
)
and the Mandelstam relation
\,s_{0}+t_{0}+u_{0}\!=0\,
.
We see that the longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitude
equals the gravitational KK Goldstone scattering amplitude
at the leading
\mathcal{O}(E_{n}^{2})
and they differ only by
subleading terms of
\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})
.
The above Eq.(
5.36
) just demonstrates
that the GRET holds for the longitudinal KK graviton scattering
amplitude and the corresponding KK Goldstone scattering amplitude
down to
\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})
under the high energy expansion,

\mathcal{M}[h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]+\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})\,.

(5.37)

It is truly impressive to see that
building upon the longitudinal-Goldstone equivalence
of the KK GAET (
5.12
) [or (
5.19
)],
we have established the corresponding
longitudinal-Goldstone equivalence
of the GRET for the amplitudes of the
longitudinal KK graviton scattering and of the gravitational
KK Goldstone scattering as in the above Eq.(
5.37
)
by using the double-copy construction.
Hence,
this demonstrates a double-copy correspondence between
the KK GAET in the compactified 5d YM gauge theory and the KK GRET
in the compactified 5d GR.

We have the following comments in order:

(i)

Impressively, we find that our reconstructed gravitational KK Goldstone

\phi_{n}\,(=h^{55}_{n})
amplitude

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

in Eqs.(
5.34b
)(
5.35
)
from the KK Goldstone
A_{5}^{an}
-amplitude

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A^{an}_{5}A^{bn}_{5}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cn}_{5}A^{dn}_{5}]

in Eqs.(
5.14b
)(
5.8a
)-(
5.8c
)
in the compactified 5d YM gauge theory via the double-copy approach
has exactly the same energy and angular dependence as what
we obtained by directly computing the
\phi_{n}
-amplitude (
4.22
) in the compactified 5d KK GR theory.
This double-copy reconstruction is naturally expected
via the correspondence

\,A_{5}^{an}\otimes\!A_{5}^{an}\!\longrightarrow h_{55}^{n}\,

where both the KK Goldstone bosons
A_{5}^{an}

and
h^{n}_{55}(=\!\phi_{n})
become effectively massless
in the high energy limit
M_{n}^{2}/E^{2}\!\rightarrow\!0
.
We note that both the leading gravitational KK Goldstone
amplitude (
5.34b
)(
5.35
)
of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})

and the leading gauge-theory KK Goldstone amplitude
(
5.14b
)(
5.8a
)-(
5.8c
) of
\mathcal{O}(E^{0})

are
mass-independent.
Hence, their structures reflect
the 5d gauge symmetry of the KK YM theory
and the 5d diffeomorphism invariance of the KK
GR theory.

(ii)

Note that the (helicity-zero) longitudinal KK gauge bosons
A_{L}^{an}

and longitudinal KK gravitons
h_{L}^{n}
do not exist in the
massless YM gauge theory or massless GR. Hence they
are truly distinctive in the KK theories.
As we observe,
the key point
is that
according to the KK GAET (section
5.1
)

[
8
]
[
9
]

and GRET (sections
3
-
4
),
the leading longitudinal scattering amplitudes of

\,A_{L}^{an}\,
and
\,h_{L}^{n}\,
equal the corresponding amplitudes
of the KK Goldstone bosons (
A_{5}^{an}
and
h_{55}^{n}
)
and are mass-independent
(corresponding to the limit
M_{n}^{2}/E^{2}\!\rightarrow\!0

under high energy expansion), despite that the longitudinal
polarization vector
\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}

(tensor
\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}
) of
A_{L}^{an}
(
h_{L}^{n}
) has
explicit mass-dependence.
This is why we can construct a similar double-copy from the
leading longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitudes of

\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{0})

to the corresponding longitudinal KK graviton amplitudes
of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})
.
The above also explains that even though the original double-copy formulation
[
24
]
[
25
]
was shown to hold
in the massless theory, we can still extend it to our current
double-copy construction for the compactified massive KK theories
to the leading order amplitude of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})
, which is

mass-independent
.
All the mass-dependent terms belong to the subleading order of

\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})
and are of the same order as the residual term
in the GRET, as we will analyze further
in sections
5.3
-
5.4
.

We stress that our double-copy construction guarantees that

the leading longitudinal KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitude
(
5.34
)-(
5.35
)
must scale as
\,\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})\,

under the high energy expansion.

According to our double-copy construction,
this
\,\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})\,
high energy behavior just
corresponds to the
\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{0})\,

leading energy behavior of the KK gauge (Goldstone) boson amplitude (
5.14
),
which are both mass-independent.

In fact, our double-copy construction
(based on the scattering amplitudes of 5d YM gauge theory
and the KK GAET
[
8
]
[
9
]
)
gives an independent proof
that the longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitudes
must have large energy-cancellations of

\mathcal{O}(E^{10})\!\rightarrow\!\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
.
We achieve this by establishing a new correspondence
between the two energy-cancellations of
the four-particle longitudinal KK
scattering amplitudes:

\,E^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}\,
in the 5d KK YM theory (YM5)
and
\,E^{10}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{2}\,
in the 5d KK GR (GR5).
Here, with the double-copy construction,
we use the first energy-cancellation of
\,E^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}\,

(YM5) to deduce the second energy-cancellation of

\,E^{10}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{2}\,
(GR5).
Thus, we may present schematically this new correspondence
between the two energy-cancellations as follows:

\displaystyle E^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}\,\mathrm{(YM5)}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Longrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ E^{10}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{2}\,\mathrm{(GR5)}\,.

(5.38)

In passing, some recent literature on the double-copy
construction for certain specific KK models
appeared
[
45
]
[
46
]
,
in which
[
45
]
briefly discussed
a scalar model compactified on

\mathbb{R}^{4}\!\times\!S^{1}

with an extra spectral condition imposed on
the KK mass-spectrum, and
[
46
]

discussed a KK inspired action
with extra global U(1) symmetry to have certain
special mass-condition for double-copy.
But these special KK models differ from the standard KK theory
with obifold
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
in our study and their methods do not
apply to our case, so they do not overlap with our current study.

(iii)

Our reconstructed gravitational KK Goldstone boson scattering
amplitude (
5.34b
)(
5.35
)
by double-copy method
is confirmed by our direct computation of the gravitational KK
Goldstone amplitude in Eq.(
4.22
),
which also equals our reconstructed (helicity-zero)
longitudinal KK graviton amplitude (
5.35
). In addition, we find that our longitudinal KK graviton
amplitude in Eq.(
5.35
) as reconstructed from
our longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude (
5.14a
)
has exactly the same energy and angular dependence as those obtained
by direct Feynman-diagram calculations of the longitudinal KK
graviton amplitudes in
Refs.
[
14
]
[
15
]
.
12
12

12

After submitting this paper to arXiv:2106.04568,
we learnt from colleagues Sekhar Chivukula and Elizabeth Simmons
via private communication that their postdoc Xing Wang also checked
that the double-copy gave the correct expression for
massive KK graviton scattering in the case of orbifolded torus.

(iv)

The amplitudes (
5.34a
)-(
5.34b
)
have no double poles, so its denominator
should be proportional to the product

\,s_{0}\hskip 1.0ptt_{0}\hskip 1.0ptu_{0}\,
,
which is permutation invariant among
(s_{0},\,t_{0},\,u_{0})
.
We note that for the elastic scattering

\,(n,n)\!\!\rightarrow\!\!(n,n)\,
,
the above amplitude should be invariant under all possible permutations,
so the structure of this amplitude should take the form of

\,(s_{0}\hskip 1.0ptt_{0}\hskip 1.0ptu_{0})^{a}(s_{0}^{2}\!+\!t_{0}^{2}\!+\!u_{0}^{2})^{b}\,

with
(a,\,b)
being certain integers.
Since the denominator of the scattering amplitude
should scale like

\,s_{0}\hskip 1.0ptt_{0}\hskip 1.0ptu_{0}\!\propto\!s_{0}^{3}\,
,
so we have
\,a=-1\,
.
Note that the whole amplitude is expected to scale
like
\mathcal{O}(s^{1})
,  so the numerator has to scale as
\mathcal{O}(s^{4})
.
This means that the only possibility for the numerator is to scale
as
\,(s_{0}^{2}+t_{0}^{2}+u_{0}^{2})^{2}\,
with
\,b\!=\!2\,
.
With these, we can generally deduce
that the kinematic structure of the amplitude
(
5.34
)
behaves as

\,(s_{0}^{2}\!+\!t_{0}^{2}\!+\!u_{0}^{2})^{2}/(s_{0}\hskip 1.0ptt_{0}\hskip 1.0ptu_{0})\,
,
which explains why our explicit construction
should lead to the formula of (
5.35c
) indeed.

(v)

The overall conversion constant
c_{0}
in
Eqs.(
5.34
)-(
5.35
)
is undetermined by the double-copy construction itself,
but is expected to be universal at least for each given spacetime dimension.
To match our double-copy result
(
5.35
)
with the gravitational KK Goldstone amplitude
(
4.22
),
we choose the following conversion constant:

c_{0}\,=\,-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\,24g^{2}\,}\,.

(5.39)

We also notice that in the traditional massless 4d theory,
the graviton amplitude reconstructed from the BCJ double-copy
can fully match the graviton amplitude in 4d massless
GR with the conversion constant

\tilde{c}_{0}\,=\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\,16g^{2}\,}\,.

(5.40)

Our definition of the group structure constant
C^{abc}
differs from
the group structure constant
f^{abc}
of Refs.
[
24
]
[
25
]
by a simple normalization factor:

C^{abc}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}f^{abc}=-\frac{\text{i}}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\text{Tr}\left([T^{a},T^{b}]\,T^{c}\right)\,,

(5.41)

where
T^{a}
is the generator of SU(N) group.

Next, we further extend the above analysis to general processes

A^{an}_{L}A^{bk}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cm}_{L}A^{d\ell}_{L}

and

A^{an}_{5}A^{bk}_{5}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cm}_{5}A^{d\ell}_{5}

including the inelastic KK scattering channels.
According to the Eqs.(
5.14a
)-(
5.14b
) and
Eq.(
5.18
),
we write the LO inelastic scattering amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}\!\left[\,\!A^{an}_{L}A^{bk}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cm}_{L}A^{d\ell}_{L}\,\right]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\zeta_{nkm\ell}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(5.42a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\!\left[\,\!A^{an}_{5}A^{bk}_{5}\!\rightarrow\!A^{cm}_{5}A^{d\ell}_{5}\,\right]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\zeta_{nkm\ell}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(5.42b)

where
\,\zeta_{nnnn}\!\!=\!\!1\,
,

\,\zeta_{nnmm}\!=\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{2}\,}{3}$}\,

for
n\!\neq\!m
,  and

\,\zeta_{nkm\ell}\!=\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{3}$}\,

for
(n,k,m,\ell)
having
no more than one equality.
Thus, using the color-kinematics duality relations
(
5.33a
)-(
5.33b
)
and up to an overall conversion constant
c_{0}
,
we can further reconstruct the
general scattering amplitude of longitudinal KK gravitons
and the scattering amplitude of the corresponding gravitational
KK Goldstone bosons by using the following relations:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\!\left[\,\!h^{n}_{L}h^{k}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{m}_{L}h^{\ell}_{L}\!\,\right]

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ \zeta_{nkm\ell}\,\mathcal{M}\!\left[\,\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\!\,\right]+\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{j}^{2})\,,

(5.43a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\!\left[\,\!\phi_{n}\phi_{k}\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{m}\phi_{\ell}\!\,\right]

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ \zeta_{nkm\ell}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]+\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{j}^{2})\,.

(5.43b)

Then, using Eq.(
5.37
),
we can deduce the GRET by double-copy reconstruction
for the general scattering process:

\mathcal{M}\!\left[\,\!h^{n}_{L}h^{k}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{m}_{L}h^{\ell}_{L}\!\,\right]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\!\left[\,\!\phi_{n}\phi_{k}\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{m}\phi_{\ell}\!\,\right]+\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{j}^{2})\,,

(5.44)

where the KK numbers of the initial and final states obey

|n\!\pm\!k|=|m\!\pm\!\ell|
.

We observe that our double-copy constructions in
Eqs.(
5.37
) and (
5.44
)
have explicitly established the KK GRET from the KK GAET
(
5.12
) and (
5.19
):

the leading amplitude of the longitudinal KK graviton
scattering equals that of the gravitational KK Goldstone scattering
at
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
(which is mass-independent)
under the high energy expansion,
and their difference is only of
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt
.

This means that in our general formulation
of the KK GRET (
3.16
)
the sum of all the
\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{n})
residual terms must be of

\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})
,
even though the naive power counting on their individual amplitudes
containing one or more external state of

\,\tilde{v}_{n}(=\tilde{v}_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}_{n})
or

\tilde{h}_{n}(=\eta_{\mu\nu}\tilde{h}_{n}^{\mu\nu})

gives
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
.
Hence, we deduce that
the double-copy construction
of the KK GRET identity (
3.15
) from the
KK GAET identity
[
9
]
in
the KK YM gauge theory provides a new mechanism of
energy cancellation from
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
down to
\mathcal{O}(E^{0})

in the sum of all the
\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{n})
residual terms on the RHS of the
KK GRET (
3.16
).

We will further demonstrate the realization
of this new energy-cancellation mechanism of

\,E^{2}\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}\,

for the residual terms of GRET in the next subsections.

Constructing Mass-Dependent KK Amplitudes
from Double-Copy

In this subsection, we study the extended double-copy construction
of the mass-dependent KK scattering amplitudes at the NLO.
We will make two types of high energy expansions by using the
expansion parameter
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt
or
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
,
where the Mandelstam variable

\,s\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pts_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+4M_{n}^{2}\,
for the four-point
elastic KK amplitudes.
As we will show,
the advantage of the
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion is that
it can automatically ensure that the LO numerators of
the KK amplitudes are mass-independent,
but then the mass-dependent NLO numerators cannot obey the
kinematic Jacobi identity even after the
generalized gauge-transformation due to

\,s_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+t_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+u_{0}=0\hskip 0.85358pt
[cf. Eq.(
A.3
)].
So additional modifications are needed.
In contrast, under the
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion the LO
numerators of KK amplitudes depend only on the

\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip 0.85358pt
and
\hskip 0.85358pt\theta\hskip 0.85358pt
(where the mass-dependence is contained
only in
\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip 0.85358pt
via
\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pts_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+4M_{n}^{2}\,
),
and we can make the gauge-transformed numerators obey the
kinematic Jacobi identity oredr by order in the

\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion due to

\,s\hskip-0.85358pt+t\hskip-0.85358pt+u=4M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
[cf. Eq.(
A.2
)].
Hence, the
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion is expected to realize
the double-copy construction more successfully.
In the following,
we will present the extended double-copy constructions for
the NLO massive KK amplitudes by using the high energy expansion
of
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt
in section
5.3.1

and the high energy expansion of
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt

in section
5.3.2
.

Double-Copy of NLO KK Amplitudes under
1\hskip-0.85358pt/\hskip-0.85358pts_{0}
Expansion

In section
5.2
,
we focused on the double-copy construction
of the KK gravitational amplitudes (
5.34a
)-(
5.34b
)
at the leading order (LO) of the high energy expansion.
For this subsection, we study the double-copy
KK amplitudes (
5.14a
)-(
5.14b
)
of the 5d KK YM gauge theory up to the next-to-leading order
(NLO) of the
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion.
For this, we extend the reconstructed KK gravitational
amplitudes (
5.34a
)-(
5.34b
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}[4h_{L}^{n}]

\displaystyle\penalty\ =\penalty\ c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s})^{2}}{s_{0}}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t})^{2}}{t_{0}}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u})^{2}}{u_{0}}\,\right]=\mathcal{M}_{0}+\delta\mathcal{M}\,,

(5.45a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[4\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle\penalty\ =\penalty\ c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s})^{2}}{s_{0}}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t})^{2}}{t_{0}}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u})^{2}}{u_{0}}\,\right]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}+\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,,

(5.45b)

where the conversion constant

\,c_{0}\!\!=\!\!-{\kappa^{2}}/(24g^{2})\,

is given by Eq.(
5.39
), as determined by matching the
corresponding leading order gravitational KK amplitude
(
4.22
).
According to Eqs.(
5.13c
)-(
5.13d
),
we expand the numerator factors
(\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})

to the NLO and naively derive the following reconstructed
subleading order gravitational KK amplitudes:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ 2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}}{s_{0}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}}{t_{0}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}}{u_{0}}\right)\!,

(5.46a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ 2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}}{s_{0}}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}}{t_{0}}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}}{u_{0}}\right)\!.

(5.46b)

We first note that the above double-copy construction should give
the correct powers of the (energy, mass)-dependence of
the corresponding NLO gravitational KK amplitudes under the
high energy expansion.
The structure of the KK amplitudes in the 5d KK YM gauge theory
has been well understood as we showed in
Eqs.(
5.4
)-(
5.9
) and
Eqs.(
5.13
)-(
5.14
)
of section
5.1
.
We see that in the 5d KK YM gauge theory,
the LO and NLO amplitudes in each channel are

(\mathcal{K}_{j}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{j}^{0})\!=\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{0})
and

(\delta\mathcal{K}_{j},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{j})\!=\mathcal{O}(M_{n}^{2}/E^{2})
.
Thus, the LO and NLO numerators are

(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0})=\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})
and

(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})=\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})
.
Hence, we generally deduce that the reconstructed double-copy
of the LO and NLO KK amplitudes for gravitational KK scattering
should have the following power-dependence on
the (energy, mass):

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}_{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0})

\displaystyle\,=\,\mathcal{O}\!\left(\!\kappa^{2}\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0})^{2}\!,\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0})^{2}\,}{s_{0j}}\!\right)=\,\mathcal{O}\!\left(\kappa^{2}E^{2}M_{n}^{0}\right)\!,

(5.47a)

\displaystyle(\delta\mathcal{M},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})

\displaystyle\,=\,\mathcal{O}\!\left(\!\kappa^{2}\frac{\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,}{s_{0j}}\!\right)=\,\mathcal{O}\!\left(\kappa^{2}E^{0}M_{n}^{2}\right)\!,

(5.47b)

where

\,s_{0j}\!\in\!(s_{0},t_{0},u_{0})\!=\!\mathcal{O}(E^{2})\,

and we have used
\,c_{0}g^{2}\sim\!\mathcal{O}(\kappa^{2})

according to Eq.(
5.39
).
The above power counting fully agrees with the explicit
calculations of the KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitudes
of the compactified 5d GR (GR5)
in Eqs.(
4.30
) and
(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
).
The above general power counting results
(
5.47a
)-(
5.47b
)
are predicted by the double-copy method based upon the
amplitude structure of the well-understood
5d KK YM gauge theory (section
5.1
).
These are important for our GRET formulation
as we will discuss further in section
5.4
.

As we noted in Eq.(
5.20
),
the NLO numerators
(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})

do not satisfy the kinematic Jacobi identity.
Thus, we expect that the reconstructed NLO amplitudes
by double-copy may not exactly reproduce the corresponding
gravitational amplitudes.
Using Eqs.(
5.8
)-(
5.9
) and
Eqs.(
5.13c
)-(
5.13d
),
we can directly compute the reconstructed NLO amplitudes
(
5.46a
)-(
5.46b
) as follows:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}}{192\,}(2050+959\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+62\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\!+c_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\,,

(5.48a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\,}{64}(494+513\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+18\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\!-c_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\,,

(5.48b)

where the conversion constant
c_{0}
is given by
Eq.(
5.39
) as determined by matching the
double-copy amplitudes with the gravitational amplitudes
at the leading order.
It is instructive to compare the above NLO double-copy amplitudes
(
5.48a
)-(
5.48b
)
with the corresponding gravitational amplitudes
(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
)
as directly computed from the 5d KK GR theory.
It is good to see that the reconstructed NLO
double-copy amplitudes (
5.48a
)-(
5.48b
)
indeed have the same kinematic structures
as that of the corresponding gravitational amplitudes
(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
)
because they all contain the angular terms
of the type

\,(1,\,c_{2\theta},\,c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\!\times\!\csc^{4}\!\theta\,

though their coefficients differ.
Their differences in the coefficients are qiute expected
because our Eq.(
5.20
) shows that
the NLO numerators
(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})

do not satisfy the kinematic Jacobi identity
even though in each channel of the NLO amplitudes
(
5.46a
)-(
5.46b
) the numerator

\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\,
or
\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,

contains product of both LO and NLO factors where
the LO factors
(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0})

still obey the kinematic Jacobi identities.
Thus, we do not expect the current BCJ-type double-copy method
would exactly hold.

Next, we further compute the differences between the
NLO amplitudes of the longitudinal KK graviton scattering and
of the KK gravitational Goldstone bosons
for the original gravitational amplitudes
(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
)
of the GR5 and
for the above reconstructed amplitudes (
5.48a
)-(
5.48b
)
by double-copy (DC)
at the NLO:

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{GR5})

\displaystyle\,=\,\delta\mathcal{M}-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,=\,-\frac{\penalty\ 3\kappa^{2}\!M_{n}^{2}\penalty\ }{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(19.5+c_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.49a)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})

\displaystyle\,=\,\delta\mathcal{M}-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,=\,\frac{\penalty\ \kappa^{2}\!M_{n}^{2}\penalty\ }{12}\hskip 0.85358pt(-69+4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}+c_{4\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.49b)

which exhibit different angular structures.

We see that the GR5 result (
5.49a
) contains
only the terms of
(1,\,c_{2\theta})
types due to rather

precise cancellations of the

(c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\times\csc^{4}\!\theta
terms
between
the amplitudes (
F.7b
) and (
F.7c
),
while the double-copy result (
5.49b
)
contains an extra non-cancelled angular term
\,c_{4\theta}\,
and
an extra overall angular factor

\,\csc^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\theta\,
.
This shows the failure of the double-copy result
(
5.49b
)
to correctly reconstruct even the structure of
(1,\,c_{2\theta})

in the original GR5 result (
5.49a
).
In fact, this precise cancellation is highly nontrivial
because after careful examination we observe that
this precise cancellation depends on
all the coefficients

in the angular structure

\,(1,\,c_{2\theta},\,c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\!\times\!\csc^{4}\!\theta\,

of both the original gravitational amplitudes
(
F.7b
) and (
F.7c
).
We find that if one changes by hand
any one of these coefficients [even for the constant term
inside the parentheses of

\,(\cdots)\!\times\csc^{4}\!\theta
]
by any small number (such as
+1
or
-1
)
in either the KK graviton amplitude (
F.7b
) or
the KK Goldstone amplitude (
F.7c
), then
it has to destroy this precise cancellation
in the amplitude-difference

\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\text{GR5})
of Eq.(
5.49a
)
and thus all the terms of

\,(1,\,c_{2\theta},\,c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\times\csc^{4}\!\theta\,

in the original amplitudes have to reappear in the difference

\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\text{GR5})
.

We can understand the failure of the correct cancellation
in the reconstructed result
\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\text{DC})

of Eq.(
5.49b
)
by noting the violation of the kinematic Jacobi identity
for the NLO numerators
(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})

as shown in Eq.(
5.20
).
In fact, by inspecting
Eqs.(
5.46a
)-(
5.46b
),
we note that for each given channel the amplitude-difference

\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})\!=\delta\mathcal{M}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,
has the numerator

\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,

which could not even be factorized into any BCJ-type product

\,X_{j}Y_{j}\,
with each factor (
X_{j}
or
Y_{j}
)
obeying the kinematic Jacobi identity separately.
Hence, it is no surprise that the reconstructed
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})

could not even reproduce the correct structure of
the original GR5 result (
5.49a
).

In the following, we will try to construct an improved
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})

in which the numerator of each channel can take the
BCJ-type product form
\,X_{j}Y_{j}\,

with each factor (
X_{j}
or
Y_{j}
)
obeying the kinematic Jacobi identity separately.
For the above purpose, we first rewrite the reconstructed
NLO KK scattering amplitudes
(
5.46a
)-(
5.46b
)
by using the relation (
5.17
):

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\,=\,\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}-8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\sum_{j}\!\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\,=\,\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}-8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\,\chi\,,

(5.50a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\,=\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}+8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\sum_{j}\!\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,=\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}+8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\,\chi\,,

(5.50b)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt\equiv\,2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\,}{s_{0j}}\,,\quad\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\hskip 0.85358pt\equiv\,2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,}{s_{0j}}\,,

(5.50c)

where
\,s_{0j}\!\in\!(s_{0},\,t_{0},\,u_{0})\,
,
and we have used Eq.(
5.20
) in the last step of
Eqs.(
5.50a
)-(
5.50b
).
It is clear that the last terms on the RHS of
Eqs.(
5.50a
)-(
5.50b
) are proportional to

\,\sum_{j}\!\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}=\sum_{j}\!\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\!=\chi\neq 0\,
,
which violate the kinematic Jacobi identity.

Then, we can compute the difference between the NLO
KK longitudinal and Goldstone amplitudes:

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{M}_{1}

\displaystyle\,\equiv\,\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,=\,2c_{0}g^{2}\sum_{j}\!\frac{\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})\,}{s_{0j}}\,=\,-\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})\,,

(5.51a)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{M}_{2}

\displaystyle\,\equiv\,\delta\mathcal{M}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\,=\,2c_{0}g^{2}\sum_{j}\!\frac{\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j})\,}{s_{0j}}\,=\,-\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})\,,

(5.51b)

where in the last steps of Eqs.(
5.51a
)-(
5.51b
),
we have computed each sum directly by using the LO and NLO
numerators of the KK gauge (Goldstone) amplitudes
(section
5.1
) as well as Eq.(
5.39
)
for the conversion constant
c_{0}\,
.
This explicit calculation shows an equality

\,\Delta\mathcal{M}_{1}\!=\Delta\mathcal{M}_{2}\,
.
We can prove this equality in a more general way.
Using Eqs.(
5.51a
)-(
5.51b
)
and Eqs.(
5.50a
)-(
5.50b
),
we reexpress the difference (
5.49b
)
of the NLO double-copy amplitudes as follows:

\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})\,=\,\delta\mathcal{M}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,=\,\Delta\mathcal{M}_{1}\!-8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\!\sum_{j}\!\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\,=\,\Delta\mathcal{M}_{2}\!-8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\!\sum_{j}\!\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,,

(5.52)

where
\,\sum_{j}\!\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\!=\sum_{j}\!\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\!=\!\chi\,

because of the equality (
5.20a
).
This leads to
\,\Delta\mathcal{M}_{1}\!=\!\Delta\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
which agrees with the explicit calculations of
Eq.(
5.51
).
Hence, we deduce

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})

\displaystyle\,=\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})-\mathbb{X}\,,

(5.53a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle\,\equiv\,8c_{0}g^{2}c_{\theta}\,\chi=\frac{2}{3}\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}(7+c_{2\theta})\cot^{2}\!\theta\,,

(5.53b)

and

\displaystyle\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})

\displaystyle\,\equiv\,\Delta\mathcal{M}_{1}=\Delta\mathcal{M}_{2}\,=\,-\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\,(7+c_{2\theta})\,.

(5.54)

It is important to note that in Eq.(
5.53a
)
we have identified and separated a special term
\mathbb{X}
from the
amplitude-difference
\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})
,
where
\,\mathbb{X}\!\propto\!\chi\,
violates the kinematic Jacobi identity
at the NLO as shown in Eq.(
5.20a
).
By doing so, we observe that the improved
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,
,
as defined in Eq.(
5.51
),
does have a good feature, namely, each numerator
of Eq.(
5.51a
) [Eq.(
5.51b
)]
just equals the product of the LO factor

\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}
(
\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\,
)
and the NLO factor
\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\!-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,
,
which satisfy separately the kinematic Jacobi identities
(
5.15
) and (
5.22
).

This is just the desired feature as required
by the conventional BCJ-type double-copy
construction
[
24
]
[
25
]
. On the other hand, the situation of
\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})

[Eq.(
5.49b
)] is different
because in each channel the numerator of

\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})

cannot be factorized into a simple product of two factors
which could hold the kinematic Jacobi identity separately.

Then, it is instructive to compare our improved
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,

[Eq.(
5.54
)] by double-copy construction
with the original gravitational amplitude-difference

\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{GR5})
[Eq.(
5.49a
)]
as computed in the compactified 5d GR.
It is impressive that our improved
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,

in Eq.(
5.54
) does have a much simpler structure
than the naive double-copy construction

\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})\,
in Eq.(
5.49b
),
because the undesired extra
\,c_{4\theta}\,
term and extra overall
factor
\,\csc^{2}\!\theta\,
of
\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})\,

fully disappear in our improved
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,
.
This comparison shows that our improved
amplitude-difference
\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,

does share the same kinematic structure of
(1,\,c_{2\theta})

as that of
\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{GR5})
in the GR5,
although their coefficients are still different.
Given the fact that the conventional BCJ approach
was formulated only for the massless gauge and gravity theories,
it is expected that for constructing the
mass-dependent
scattering amplitudes
such as the NLO amplitudes
of our 5d KK theories, the conventional BCJ approach
would not exactly work.
Nevertheless, we have shown that our reconstructed
KK longitudinal graviton and Goldstone scattering amplitudes
(
5.48a
)-(
5.48b
)
indeed exhibit the
same kinematic structure

\,(1,\,c_{2\theta},\,c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\!\times\!\csc^{4}\!\theta\,

as that of the corresponding gravitational KK amplitudes
(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
).

Furthermore, the double-copy reconstruction of the
KK amplitude-difference at the NLO is much more nontrivial
because the original gravitational amplitude-difference

\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{GR5})
[Eq.(
5.49a
)]
contains very
precise cancellations of the terms

\,(c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\!\times\csc^{4}\!\theta

between the amplitudes
(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
).
The naive double-copy construction of the NLO amplitude-difference

\,\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{DC})\,
[Eq.(
5.49b
)]
fails to reproduce the correct kinematic structure
of the
\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{GR5})
.
But, it is impressive that
after we properly define the improved amplitude-difference

\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,

as in Eq.(
5.54
) and Eqs.(
5.51a
)-(
5.51b
)
by removing the Jacobi-violating term and
ensuring its numerator in each channel
factorized into product factors (obeying
the kinematic Jacobi identities respectively),
we find that the improved double-copy result

\,\Delta\hbox to0.0pt{\;\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}(\mathrm{DC})\,

[Eq.(
5.54
)]
does exhibit the
same kinematic structure

as that of the original gravitational amplitude-difference

\Delta\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{GR5})
[Eq.(
5.49a
)].
This is an encouraging evidence showing that
as long as the BCJ-type numerators can be properly improved
to satisfy the kinematic Jacobi identities,
such a double-copy approach is still quite meaningful
to certain extent,
predicting the correct structure of
the corresponding gravitational amplitudes and
the (energy, mass)-dependence up to NLO,

even for the mass-dependent amplitudes.

In the rest of this subsection, we will attempt to make an
improved double-copy construction of the NLO KK amplitudes
and reproduce the original NLO KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitudes
(
5.48a
)-(
5.48b
)
by following our proposal of the improved NLO numerators

(\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime})
in Eq.(
5.25
)
which have the desired property of
satisfying the kinematic Jacobi identities
(
5.28a
)-(
5.28b
).
Moreover, the corresponding improved NLO KK
longitudinal and Goldstone amplitudes

\,(\delta\mathcal{T}_{L}^{\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{5}^{\prime})
still obey
the KK GAET identity (
5.31
) which reflects
the KK YM gauge symmetry.

Using the improved KK gauge (Goldstone) boson amplitudes
(
5.27a
)-(
5.27b
), we construct
the following new NLO gravitational KK amplitudes by double-copy:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ 2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime}}{s_{0}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime}}{t_{0}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0}\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime}}{u_{0}}\right)\!,

(5.55a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ 2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\prime}}{s_{0}}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\prime}}{t_{0}}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\prime}}{u_{0}}\right)\!,

(5.55b)

where the improved NLO numerators

\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime}\!=\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}\!-\chi_{j}\,

and
\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime}\!=\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\!-\widetilde{\chi}_{j}\,

as defined in Eq.(
5.25
).
Since the NLO gravitational KK amplitudes
(
5.48a
)-(
5.48b
)
only contain angular factors

\cos m\theta
(with
m=2,4,6
) and

\csc^{4}\!\theta\!=\!1/\sin^{4}\!\theta\,

which are invariant under
\,\theta\!\rightarrow\!\pi-\theta
,
we may choose the decomposition terms
in Eq.(
5.24
) as

(\chi_{s},\,\chi_{t},\,\chi_{u})=(\chi,\,z,\,-z)

and

\,(\widetilde{\chi}_{s},\,\widetilde{\chi}_{t},\,\widetilde{\chi}_{u})=(\chi,\,\tilde{z},\,-\tilde{z})
,
where
\,\chi\,
is given by Eq.(
5.20b
).
Thus, using Eq.(
5.25
) we express
the improved NLO numerators as follows:

\displaystyle(\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime},\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime},\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,=\,(\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}\!-\!\chi,\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}\!-\!z,\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}\!+\!z\,)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.56a)

\displaystyle(\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,=\,(\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}\!-\!\chi,\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}\!-\!\tilde{z},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}\!+\!\tilde{z}\,)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(5.56b)

The new parameters
(z,\,\tilde{z})
are functions of
\,\theta\,

and will be determined by matching the reconstructed
NLO KK amplitudes
(\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime\prime})

in Eq.(
5.55a
)-(
5.55b
)
with the original NLO KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitudes

(\delta\mathcal{M},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})

in Eqs.(
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
)
of the 5d KK GR:

\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}=\,\delta\mathcal{M}\,,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime\prime}=\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\,.

(5.57)

Thus, we can solve the parameters
\,(z,\,\tilde{z})

from Eq.(
5.57
) as follows:

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{\,M_{n}^{2}\left(614+371c_{2\theta}\!+42c_{4\theta}\!-3c_{6\theta}\right)\,}{16\left(7\!+c_{2\theta}\right)\sin^{2}\!\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.58a)

\displaystyle\tilde{z}

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{\,M_{n}^{2}(1666-\!1025c_{2\theta}\!+382c_{4\theta}\!+c_{6\theta})\,}{16\left(7\!+c_{2\theta}\right)\sin^{2}\!\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(5.58b)

Finally, by substituting the improved NLO numerators
(
5.56
) with Eqs.(
5.58a
)-(
5.58b
)
into Eqs.(
5.55a
)-(
5.55b
),
we obtain the reconstructed NLO KK amplitudes:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\text{(DC)}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\,}{128}\left(650+261\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}+102c_{4\theta}+11\hskip 0.85358ptc_{6\theta}\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.59a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime\prime}\text{(DC)}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\,}{128}(-706+2049\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}-318\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}-c_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.59b)

which reproduce precisely
the original NLO gravitational KK amplitudes

\,(\delta\mathcal{M},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})
in
Eqs. (
F.7b
)-(
F.7c
)
of the 5d KK GR theory, as expected. This gives a consistency check of the above analysis.

In passing, it would be useful to extend our
present LO and NLO analyses to the scattering processes
with five or more external particles in our future work.
We also note that the original BCJ conjecture was inspired
by the KLT relation that connects the amplitudes of
the massless gravity theory
to that of the massless YM gauge theory.
The KLT kernal may be further reinterpreted as
the inverse amplitude of a bi-adjoint scalar field theory
[
47
]
.
In Appedix
G
, we will extend the
KLT double-copy approach for constructing the four-particle
KK graviton amplitudes
and demonstrate the consistency with the above
improved BCJ construction.

Improved Double-Copy of NLO KK Amplitudes under
1\hskip-0.85358pt/\hskip-0.85358pts
Expansion

In this subsection, we present an improved double-copy construction
of the KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitudes from the KK gauge boson
(Goldstone) amplitudes under the high energy expansion of

\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
. With this we can construct improved numerators for
the KK gauge boson (Goldstone) amplitudes which can fully satisfy
the kinematic Jacobi identity.
We will show that this improved massive double-copy approach
is better than the double-copy method by using the

\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion
as we gave in the previous subsection
5.3.1
.

In the following, we make the high energy expansion of

\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
, where
\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip 0.85358pt
is the conventional
Mandelstam variable and
\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pts_{0}+4M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt

holds for the four-particle elastic scattering.
We use the notations

\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s},\,\bar{s}_{0})=(s,\,s_{0})/M_{n}^{2}\,
and thus

\,\bar{s}\!=\!\bar{s}_{0}+4\,
. For the exact tree-level
KK longitudinal gauge boson amplitude
\,\mathcal{T}[4A_{L}^{n}]

and the KK Goldstone boson amplitude

\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[4A_{5}^{n}]
in Eq.(
5.4
),
we can reexpress their kinematic factors (
5.5
)
in terms of the conventional Mandelstam variable

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{\,(4\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!5\bar{s}\!-\!8)c_{\theta}\,}{2\bar{s}}\,,\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{\,(3\bar{s}\!-\!8)c_{\theta}\,}{2\bar{s}}\,,

(5.60a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{\,Q_{0}\!+Q_{1}c_{\theta}\!+Q_{2}c_{2\theta}\!+Q_{3}c_{3\theta}\,}{\,2(\bar{s}\!-\!4)[(3\bar{s}\!+\!4)\!+\!4\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\!-\!4)c_{2\theta}]\,}\,,\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\frac{\,\widetilde{Q}_{0}\!+\widetilde{Q}_{1}c_{\theta}\!+\widetilde{Q}_{2}c_{2\theta}\,}{\,2(\bar{s}\!-\!4)[(3\bar{s}\!+\!4)\!+\!4\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\!-\!4)c_{2\theta}]\,}\,,

(5.60b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\frac{\,Q_{0}\!-Q_{1}c_{\theta}\!+Q_{2}c_{2\theta}\!-Q_{3}c_{3\theta}\,}{\,2(\bar{s}\!-\!4)[(3\bar{s}\!+\!4)\!-\!4\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\!-\!4)c_{2\theta}]\,}\,,\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{\,\widetilde{Q}_{0}\!-\widetilde{Q}_{1}c_{\theta}\!+\widetilde{Q}_{2}c_{2\theta}\,}{\,2(\bar{s}\!-\!4)[(3\bar{s}\!+\!4)\!-\!4\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\!-\!4)c_{2\theta}]\,}\,,

(5.60c)

where the functions
\{Q_{j},\widetilde{Q}_{j}\}
are given by

\displaystyle Q_{0}=8\bar{s}^{3}\!-\!63\bar{s}^{2}\!+\!72\bar{s}\!+\!80\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{0}=15\bar{s}^{2}\!+\!24\bar{s}\!-\!80\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.61)

\displaystyle Q_{1}=2(7\bar{s}^{3}\!-\!44\bar{s}^{2}\!+\!80\bar{s}\!-\!64)\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{1}=4(3\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!20\bar{s}\!+\!32)\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle Q_{2}=8\bar{s}^{3}\!-\!45\bar{s}^{2}\!+\!8\bar{s}\!+\!48\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{2}=-3(\bar{s}\!-\!4)^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle Q_{3}=2\bar{s}(\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!10\bar{s}\!+\!24)\hskip 0.85358pt.

Then, we make the
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
expansion
for Eq.(
5.60
) and derive the LO expressions,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,5c_{\theta}\,}{2}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,3c_{\theta}\,}{2}\,,

(5.62a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,13\!+\!5c_{\theta}\!+\!4c_{2\theta}\,}{2(1\!+\!c_{\theta})\,}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,3(3\!-\!c_{\theta})\,}{2(1\!+\!c_{\theta})\,}\,,

(5.62b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,13\!-\!5c_{\theta}\!+\!4c_{2\theta}\,}{2(1\!-\!c_{\theta})\,}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,3(3\!+\!c_{\theta})\,}{2(1\!-\!c_{\theta})\,}\,,

(5.62c)

and the NLO expressions,

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{K}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{\theta}}{\bar{s}}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{\theta}}{\bar{s}}\,,

(5.63a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{K}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,4(2\!-\!3c_{\theta}\!-\!2c_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{3\theta})\,}{(1\!+\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,\bar{s}\,}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{16\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}}{(1\!+\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,\bar{s}}\,,

(5.63b)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{K}_{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,4(2\!+\!3c_{\theta}\!-\!2c_{2\theta}\!+\!c_{3\theta})\,}{(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,\bar{s}\,}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{16\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}}{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,\bar{s}\,}\,.

(5.63c)

We further define the BCJ-type numerators:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{j}=s_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{j}=\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\!+\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}=s_{j}(\mathcal{K}_{j}^{0}\!+\delta\mathcal{K}_{j})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.64a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}=s_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\!+\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}=s_{j}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{j}^{0}\!+\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{j})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.64b)

where the subscripts
\,j\!\in\!(s,\hskip 0.85358ptt,\hskip 0.85358ptu)\,
and

\,s_{j}\!\in\!(s,\hskip 0.85358ptt,\hskip 0.85358ptu)\,
.
With these, we reformulate the amplitudes (
5.4
)
in the following forms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn}A_{L}^{b\hskip 0.85358ptn}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!A_{L}^{c\hskip 0.85358ptn}A_{L}^{d\hskip 0.85358ptn}]\,=\penalty\ \,

\displaystyle g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}\,}{s}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}\,}{t}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\,\mathcal{N}_{u}\,}{u}\!\right)\!,

(5.65a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A_{5}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn}A_{5}^{b\hskip 0.85358ptn}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!A_{5}^{c\hskip 0.85358ptn}A_{5}^{d\hskip 0.85358ptn}]\,=\penalty\ \,

\displaystyle g^{2}\!\left(\!\hskip-1.42262pt\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}\,}{s}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}\,}{t}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}\,}{u}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\right)\!.

(5.65b)

We note that the newly formed LO numerators

\hskip 0.85358pt\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\}

and NLO numerators

\hskip 0.85358pt\{\delta\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\}

are mass-dependent (through
\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip 0.85358pt
), and
their sums violate the kinematic Jacobi identity:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}=10\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}M_{n}^{2}\,,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}=-6\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}M_{n}^{2}\,,

(5.66a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}=-\frac{\,M_{n}^{2}\,}{2\bar{s}}[13\bar{s}\!-\!496\!-\!12\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!-\!(\bar{s}\!+\!16)c_{4\theta}]c_{\theta}\csc^{4}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,,

(5.66b)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}=-\frac{\,M_{n}^{2}\,}{2\bar{s}}[13\bar{s}\!-\!448\!-\!4(3\bar{s}\!+\!16)c_{2\theta}\!-\!\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}]c_{\theta}\csc^{4}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,.

(5.66c)

The violation of the kinematic Jacobi identity
at both the LO and NLO shows that
the conventional BCJ double-copy method of the massless gauge theories
cannot be naively applied to the case of the elastic scattering
amplitudes of KK gauge (Goldstone) bosons.
But, we observe that the amplitudes (
5.65
)
are invariant under the generalized gauge transformations
of the kinematic numerators:

\mathcal{N}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{\prime}=\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}+s_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}\hskip 0.85358pt\Delta\,,\hskip 22.76219pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{\prime}=\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}+s_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\Delta}\,.

(5.67)

Thus, we can realize the kinematic Jacobi identities
for the gauge-transformed numerators

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=0\,,\hskip 22.76219pt\sum_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=0\,,

(5.68)

by generally solving the gauge parameters

(\Delta,\,\widetilde{\Delta})

for the elastic amplitudes as follows:

\Delta=-\frac{1}{\,4M_{n}^{2}\,}\!\sum_{j}\mathcal{N}_{j}\,,\hskip 22.76219pt\widetilde{\Delta}=-\frac{1}{\,4M_{n}^{2}\,}\!\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\,.\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

(5.69)

Expanding both sides of Eq.(
5.69
), we derive the gauge parameters

(\Delta,\,\widetilde{\Delta})=(\Delta_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\Delta_{1},\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\Delta}_{1})\,

at the LO and NLO:

\displaystyle\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,4\,}(9\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt7\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})c_{\theta}\csc^{2}\!\theta\,,\qquad

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,4\,}(17\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})c_{\theta}\csc^{2}\!\theta\,,

(5.70a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,(62c_{\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt5c_{3\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{5\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\,}{\bar{s}}\,,\qquad

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,(15c_{\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{3\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\,}{\bar{s}}\,.

(5.70b)

Using Eqs.(
5.67
) and (
5.70a
),
we derive the gauge-transformed LO numerators

(\mathcal{N}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})
,
which are
equal
to each other:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}

\displaystyle=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}=\,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\,2\,}$}\hskip 0.85358pts\hskip 0.85358pt(7\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\csc^{2}\!\theta\,,

(5.71a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}

\displaystyle=\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}=\,-\frac{\penalty\ s\hskip 0.85358pt(42\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-1.42262pt15\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt6\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{3\theta})\penalty\ }{16\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})}\,,\hskip 14.22636pt

(5.71b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}

\displaystyle=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}=\,\frac{\penalty\ s\hskip 0.85358pt(42\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-1.42262pt15\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt6\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{3\theta})\penalty\ }{16\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})}\,.

(5.71c)

We see that the LO numerators

(\mathcal{N}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})

are of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})
and the LO equality holds:

\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\!=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\hskip 0.85358pt
.
Hence, we deduce the
equivalence
between the two LO amplitudes

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pt0}^{\,\prime}[4A^{n}_{L}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\hskip 0.85358pt0}^{\,\prime}[4A_{5}^{n}]\,,

(5.72)

at the
\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{0})
,
which is an explicit realization of the KK GAET
[
8
]
.

Then, substituting Eq.(
5.70b
) into
Eq.(
5.67
), we further derive the gauge-transformed NLO
numerators
\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{\prime}\,

for the elastic KK gauge boson amplitude:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{s}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\,4\,}$}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(246\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!7\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\!+\!3\hskip 0.85358ptc_{5\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\,,

(5.73a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{\penalty\ M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(131\!-\!8c_{\theta}\!-\!4c_{2\theta}\!+\!8c_{3\theta}\!+\!c_{4\theta})\penalty\ }{8\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}}\,,

(5.73b)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{u}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{\penalty\ M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(131\!+\!8c_{\theta}\!-\!4c_{2\theta}\!-\!8c_{3\theta}\!+\!c_{4\theta})\penalty\ }{8\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\!c_{\theta})^{2}}\,,

(5.73c)

and the gauge-transformed NLO numerators

\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}^{\prime}\,

for the corresponding KK Goldstone boson amplitude:

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}_{s}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\,4\,}$}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(238\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!19\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\!-\!c_{5\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\!\theta\,,

(5.74a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{\penalty\ M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(99\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!28c_{2\theta}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{4\theta})\penalty\ }{8\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}}\,,

(5.74b)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}_{u}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{\penalty\ M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(99\!-\!8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!28\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+\!8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{4\theta})\penalty\ }{8\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\!c_{\theta})^{2}}\,.

(5.74c)

We see that these NLO numerators
are of
\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})
, which are mass-dependent.
It is straightforward to verify explicitly that the gauge-transformed
numerators (
5.71
) and
(
5.73
)-(
5.74
) obey the
kinematic Jacobi identities (
5.68
)
at the LO and NLO, respectively.

Since the above gauge-transformed numerators hold the
kinematic Jacobi identities (
5.68
),
they are expected to realize the
color-kinematics duality order by order.
Thus, we construct the following extended BCJ-type
massive double-copy formulas for the elastic scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons and KK Goldstone bosons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[4h_{L}^{n}]

\displaystyle\,=\,c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime})^{2}}{s}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime})^{2}}{t}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime})^{2}}{u}\,\right]=\,\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}+\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\,,

(5.75a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}[4\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle\,=\,c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\prime})^{2}}{s}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\prime})^{2}}{t}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\prime})^{2}}{u}\,\right]=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime}+\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\,,

(5.75b)

where the LO and NLO KK gravitational amplitudes are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\,=\,c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})^{2}}{s}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})^{2}}{t}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})^{2}}{u}\,\right]\!,

(5.76a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\,=\,c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left[\,\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})^{2}}{s}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})^{2}}{t}+\frac{(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime})^{2}}{u}\,\right]\!,

(5.76b)

and

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ 2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime}}{s}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime}}{t}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime}}{u}\right)\!,

(5.77a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\,=\penalty\ 2c_{0}\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\prime}}{s}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\prime}}{t}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\prime}}{u}\right)\!.

(5.77b)

In the above the conversion constant

\,c_{0}\!=\!-{\kappa^{2}}/(24g^{2})\,

is given by Eq.(
5.39
).

Using the double-copy formulas in
Eqs.(
5.76
)-(
5.77
)
and
the gauge-transformed numerators

(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime})

in Eqs.(
5.71
)-(
5.74
),
we construct the following
LO elastic KK graviton and KK Goldstone amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})=\frac{\,3\kappa^{2}\,}{\,128\,}\frac{\penalty\ (7\!+\cos 2\theta)^{2}\,}{\sin^{2}\!\theta}\hskip 0.85358pts\,,

(5.78)

and NLO elastic KK graviton and KK Goldstone amplitudes:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\penalty\ 5\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\penalty\ }{768}(1642\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt297\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt102\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt7\hskip 0.85358ptc_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,,

(5.79a)

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\penalty\ \kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\penalty\ }{768}(6386\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt3837\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt30\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt13\hskip 0.85358ptc_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{4}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,,

(5.79b)

The above Eq.(
5.78
)
shows that the reconstructed LO KK amplitude

\,\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime})\,

equals the LO KK Goldstone amplitude (
4.22b
)
and the corresponding LO longitudinal KK graviton
amplitude
[
14
]
[
15
]
. It is impressive that with Eq.(
5.78
)
we have proven explicitly the KK GRET relation

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}(\text{DC})\!=\!\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime}(\text{DC})\hskip 0.85358pt

from the KK GAET realtion

\,\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pt0}[4A^{n}_{L}]\!=\!\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\hskip 0.85358pt0}[4A_{5}^{n}]\,

by using the double-copy construction.
Note that we deduced the GRET relation
\,\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}\!=\!\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime}

earlier in Eq.(
4.25
)
based on the direct Feynman-diagram calculations.
Because our KK GAET relation
\,\mathcal{T}_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\,

generally holds for
N
-point longitudinal KK gauge (Goldstone)
amplitudes
[
8
]
[
9
]
,
we can make double-copy on both sides of

\,\mathcal{T}_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\,

and establish the GRET,

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})\!=\!\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})\hskip 0.85358pt
,
for
N
-point longitudinal KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitudes.

We can further compute the gravitational residual term of the GRET
from the difference between the two NLO amplitudes
(
5.79a
) and (
5.79b
):

\Delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathrm{DC})=\delta\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\text{DC})-\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}(\text{DC})=-\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\,(7\!+c_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(5.80)

We see that the above reconstructed residual term (
5.80
)
by the extended double-copy approach
does give the same size of
\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})

and takes the same angular structure of
(1,\,c_{2\theta})

as the original residual term
(
F.8
) although their numerical coefficients
still differ.
It is impressive to note that
Eq.(
5.80
) also demonstrates a very precise cancellation
between the angular structures

(1,\,c_{2\theta},\,c_{4\theta},\,c_{6\theta})\!\times\!\csc^{4}\!\theta\,

of the NLO double-copied KK amplitudes
(
5.79a
)-(
5.79b
)
down to the much simpler angular structure

(1,\,c_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt
. This is the same kind of angular cancellations
as what we found for the original NLO KK
graviton-Goldstone amplitudes
(
F.6b
)-(
F.6c
) and
their difference (
F.8
).
This shows that the above double-copied NLO KK amplitudes
have captured the essential features of the original KK
graviton-Goldstone amplitudes at both the LO and NLO.

Finally, as a comparison, we also note that the above
NLO amplitude-difference (
5.80
) does agree
to our earlier independent derivation of
Eq.(
5.54
) which was deduced under the
high energy expansion of
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt

and by further removing the Jacobi-violating terms
in the NLO amplitude-difference (
5.51
)
with the improved NLO amplitudes (
5.50
)
by double-copy. We stress that the key advantage of the
current double-copy approach under
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt

expansion is that our gauge-transformed numerators

(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime})

in Eqs.(
5.67
)(
5.69
)
are generally guaranteed to obey the kinematic Jacobi identities
(
5.68
) under the
high energy expansion of
\hskip 0.85358pt1\hskip-0.85358pt/s\hskip 0.85358pt
.
Hence they are expected to naturally realize the
color-kinematics duality order by order.

The above extended NLO double-copy results of
the NLO KK amplitudes
(
5.79a
)-(
5.79b
)
and their difference (
5.80
) are very encouraging,
because this construction

uses only the pure longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude

and can already give
the correct structure

of the NLO KK amplitudes including
the precise
cancellations
of the angular dependence from
Eqs.(
5.79a
)-(
5.79b
)
to Eq.(
5.80
). We have made the gauge-transformed numerators

(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime})

obey the kinematic Jacobi identities, which is a necessary
condition for realizing color-kinematics duality,
although not yet sufficient. To further construct the precise KK graviton (Goldstone)
amplitudes at the NLO and beyond, we propose another improved double-copy method, which also uses
the amplitudes of
pure longitudinal KK gauge bosons (KK Goldstone bosons) alone,

hence it is practically simple and valuable. For this, we propose the following improved BCJ-respecting
numerators at the NLO:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt(\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime\prime},\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime\prime},\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\,=\,(\delta\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime},\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime}\!-\!z,\,\delta\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime}\!+\!z\,)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.81a)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt(\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\prime\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\prime\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\,=\,(\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\prime}\!-\!\tilde{z},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\prime}\!+\!\tilde{z}\,)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.81b)

where the new parameters

(z,\,\tilde{z})
are functions of
\,\theta\,

and can be determined by matching our improved NLO KK amplitudes
of double-copy with the original NLO KK graviton (Goldstone)
amplitudes of the GR5. We stress that the modified numerators (
5.56
)
still respect the Jacobi identity because

\,\sum\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\sum\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt0\,

and

\sum\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime\prime}\!=\!\sum\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=0\,
.
Then, we can solve the parameters
(z,\,\tilde{z})
as follows:

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{\,M_{n}^{2}(1390\!+\!603\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+\!66\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\!-11c_{6\theta})\,}{12(13\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{4\theta})}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(5.82a)

\displaystyle\tilde{z}

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{\,M_{n}^{2}(4546\!-\!3585\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+\!1086\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\!+c_{6\theta})\,}{12\left(13\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{4\theta}\right)}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(5.82b)

Since the corresponding NLO KK gauge (Goldstone) boson
amplitudes
(\delta\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime},\,\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime})
are corrected only by terms of NLO,
we can still retain the general GAET identity

\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}\!\!=\!\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}\!+\!\mathcal{T}_{v}^{\prime\prime}

by redefining the residual term as

\mathcal{T}_{v}^{\prime\prime}\,=\,\mathcal{T}_{v}\!-g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\,}{t}-\frac{\mathcal{C}_{u}}{u}\!\right)\!(z\!-\!\tilde{z})\,.

(5.83)

Using Eqs.(
5.81
)-(
5.82
),
we can reproduce the exact NLO
gravitational KK amplitudes
(
F.6b
) and (
F.6c
) at tree level.
We can further apply this double-copy procedure to
higher orders (beyond NLO) when needed.

Exact NLO Double-Copy based on KK String Approach

In this subsection, we will construct an exact double-copy of
the KK graviton scattering amplitudes at the NLO based on
our recent first principle approach
of the KK string theory
[
57
]
. We note that the above improved NLO double-copy constructions in
Eqs.(
5.79
)-(
5.80
)
can already give
the correct structure

of the NLO KK amplitudes including
the precise cancellations

of the angular dependence as in Eq.(
5.49a
). These are indeed truly encouraging, and provide strong evidence
that our massive KK double-copy approach is on the right track. The importance of the current approach is twofold: (i). In practice, this KK double-copy construction is realized

under high energy expansion
. Thus, the LO double-copy
is the most important part, with which we newly establish
the GRET relation
\mathcal{M}_{0}\!=\!\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}

[Eq.(
5.78
)]
from the GAET relation

\,\mathcal{T}_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\,
[Eq.(
5.72
)],
as will be stressed in Eq.(
5.102
). We use the NLO KK graviton amplitudes only for

estimating the size
of the residual term

\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}
of the GRET (
3.16
). So the precise form of
\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}\!
is not needed
for the GRET
except to justify its size

\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}\!\!=\mathcal{O}(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})

by our double-copy construction [cf. Eq.(
5.101
)]. Hence, for our GRET formulation under the high energy limit,

the residual term
\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}\!

does belong to the NLO amplitudes and is thus neligible.
(ii). The current KK double-copy approach makes the
first serious attempt to construct the massive KK graviton amplitudes.
In general, this provides strong motivation and important guideline
for realizing the exact massive double-copy.
